"content" : "Create an instance for the following domain model:\n<domain_model>\nmodel HotelManagement\n\nclass Booking\nattributes\n    startDate : String\n    endDate : String\n    canceled : Boolean\n    bookingId : Integer\n    confirmed : Boolean\nend\n\nclass Bill\nattributes\n    price : Real\n    billId : Integer\nend\n\nclass FreeRoomTypesDTO\nattributes\n    roomTypeDescription : String\n    numBeds : Integer\n    pricePerNight : Real\n    numFreeRooms : Integer\nend\n\nclass Customer\nattributes\n    firstName : String\n    lastName : String\nend\n\nclass RoomReservation\nattributes\n    checkInDate : String\n    checkOutDate : String\nend\n\nclass RoomExtra\nattributes\n    price : Real\n    description : String\nend\n\nclass Room\nattributes\n    numBeds : Integer\n    pricePerNight : Real\n    occupied : Boolean\n    blocked : Boolean\n    roomNumber : Integer\nend\n\nassociation BookingBill between\n    Booking [1]\n    Bill [0..*] role bills\nend\n\nassociation BookingCustomer between\n    Booking [1]\n    Customer [1] role customer\nend\n\nassociation BookingRoomReservation between\n    Booking [1]\n    RoomReservation [*] role roomReservations\nend\n\nassociation RoomReservationExtras between\n    RoomReservation [1]\n    RoomExtra [*] role roomExtras\nend\n\nassociation RoomReservationRoom between\n    RoomReservation [1]\n    Room [*] role room\nend\n\nassociation BillRoomReservation between\n    Bill [1]\n    RoomReservation [1] role roomReservation\nend\n\nconstraints\ncontext Bill inv positiveBill:\n    self.price >= 0\n\ncontext Bill inv uniqueBillId:\n    Bill.allInstances->isUnique(b | b.billId)\n\ncontext Room inv positiveNumBeds:\n    self.numBeds > 0\n</domain_model>\n"
} ],
"stream" : false

So it can be used as follows:\n!store4.location := Location(14.0, 289.0)\n\n-- Enums usage:\n    -- enum Type { Clothes, Shoes }\n-- Can be used as follows:\n!store4.type := #Clothes\n</syntax_reference>\n"

"content" : "Given a conceptual model expressed in the UML-based Specification Environment (USE), your task is to generate valid and realistic instances that conform to the provided model.\n<requirements>\n- Instances must be syntactically correct according to the USE syntax_reference.\n- Avoid unnecessary comments and output the instance in plain text (i.e., not markdown).\n- Make sure instances fulfill all the model's constraints, and that multiplicities, relationships, and attributes are valid and realistic.\n- Provide multiple instances with diverse data values and structure.\n</requirements>\n\n<syntax_reference>\nHere there is a snippet showing how to create objects and set values in the specific .soil language required:\n-- This is a comment example\n\n-- Primitive data types:\n    -- Integer i.e. 1, 2, 3, etc.\n    -- Real i.e. 1.0, 21.89, 322.05556, etc.\n    -- Boolean i.e. true or false\n    -- String i.e. 'Hello World'\n\n-- You can create instances with the following syntax:\n!new <instance type>('<instance name>')\n-- Example:\n!new Client('client1')\n!new Store('store4')\n\n-- You can assign values to attributes for a created instance with the following syntax:\n!<instance name>.<attribute name> := <value>\n-- Example for different data types:\n!client1.clientId := 1 -- For Integer\n!client1.balance := 1123.45 -- For Real\n!client1.name := 'John' -- For Strings\n!store4.available := true -- For Boolean\n\n-- You can create associations between instances with the following syntax:\n!insert (<instance name1>, <instance name2>) into <association name>\n-- Example:\n!insert (client1, store4) into ClientStore\n\n-- Custom data types usage:\n    -- dataType Location\n    -- operations\n    --   Location(x : Real, y : Real)\n    --   some other operations\n    -- end\n-- You can create custom data types by calling the constructor directly; in this case, the constructor of Location requires two arguments: x and y of type Real. So it can be used as follows:\n!store4.location := Location(14.0, 289.0)\n\n-- Enums usage:\n    -- enum Type { Clothes, Shoes }\n-- Can be used as follows:\n!store4.type := #Clothes\n</syntax_reference>\n"

"content" : "Given a conceptual model expressed in the UML-based Specification Environment (USE), your task is to generate valid and realistic instances that conform to the provided model.\n<requirements>\n- Instances must be syntactically correct according to the USE syntax_reference.\n- Avoid unnecessary comments and output the instance in plain text (i.e., not markdown).\n- Make sure instances fulfill all the model's constraints, and that multiplicities, relationships, and attributes are valid and realistic.\n- Provide multiple instances with diverse data values and structure.\n</requirements>\n\n<syntax_reference>\nHere there is a snippet showing how to create objects and set values in the specific .soil language required:\n-- This is a comment example\n\n-- Primitive data types:\n    -- Integer i.e. 1, 2, 3, etc.\n    -- Real i.e. 1.0, 21.89, 322.05556, etc.\n    -- Boolean i.e. true or false\n    -- String i.e. 'Hello World'\n\n-- You can create instances with the following syntax:\n!new <instance type>('<instance name>')\n-- Example:\n!new Client('client1')\n!new Store('store4')\n\n-- You can assign values to attributes for a created instance with the following syntax:\n!<instance name>.<attribute name> := <value>\n-- Example for different data types:\n!client1.clientId := 1 -- For Integer\n!client1.balance := 1123.45 -- For Real\n!client1.name := 'John' -- For Strings\n!store4.available := true -- For Boolean\n\n-- You can create associations between instances with the following syntax:\n!insert (<instance name1>, <instance name2>) into <association name>\n-- Example:\n!insert (client1, store4) into ClientStore\n\n-- Custom data types usage:\n    -- dataType Location\n    -- operations\n    --   Location(x : Real, y : Real)\n    --   some other operations\n    -- end\n-- You can create custom data types by calling the constructor directly; in this case, the constructor of Location requires two arguments: x and y of type Real. So it can be used as follows:\n!store4.location := Location(14.0, 289.0)\n\n-- Enums usage:\n    -- enum Type { Clothes, Shoes }\n-- Can be used as follows:\n!store4.type := #Clothes\n</syntax_reference>\n"

"content" : "Given a conceptual model expressed in the UML-based Specification Environment (USE), your task is to generate valid and realistic instances that conform to the provided model.\n<requirements>\n- Instances must be syntactically correct according to the USE syntax_reference.\n- Avoid unnecessary comments and output the instance in plain text (i.e., not markdown).\n- Make sure instances fulfill all the model's constraints, and that multiplicities, relationships, and attributes are valid and realistic.\n- Provide multiple instances with diverse data values and structure.\n</requirements>\n\n<syntax_reference>\nHere there is a snippet showing how to create objects and set values in the specific .soil language required:\n-- This is a comment example\n\n-- Primitive data types:\n    -- Integer i.e. 1, 2, 3, etc.\n    -- Real i.e. 1.0, 21.89, 322.05556, etc.\n    -- Boolean i.e. true or false\n    -- String i.e. 'Hello World'\n\n-- You can create instances with the following syntax:\n!new <instance type>('<instance name>')\n-- Example:\n!new Client('client1')\n!new Store('store4')\n\n-- You can assign values to attributes for a created instance with the following syntax:\n!<instance name>.<attribute name> := <value>\n-- Example for different data types:\n!client1.clientId := 1 -- For Integer\n!client1.balance := 1123.45 -- For Real\n!client1.name := 'John' -- For Strings\n!store4.available := true -- For Boolean\n\n-- You can create associations between instances with the following syntax:\n!insert (<instance name1>, <instance name2>) into <association name>\n-- Example:\n!insert (client1, store4) into ClientStore\n\n-- Custom data types usage:\n    -- dataType Location\n    -- operations\n    --   Location(x : Real, y : Real)\n    --   some other operations\n    -- end\n-- You can create custom data types by calling the constructor directly; in this case, the constructor of Location requires two arguments: x and y of type Real. So it can be used as follows:\n!store4.location := Location(14.0, 289.0)\n\n-- Enums usage:\n    -- enum Type { Clothes, Shoes }\n-- Can be used as follows:\n!store4.type := #Clothes\n</syntax_reference>\n"

"content" : "Create an instance for the following domain model:\n<domain_model>\nmodel HotelManagement\n\nclass Booking\nattributes\n    startDate : String\n    endDate : String\n    canceled : Boolean\n    bookingId : Integer\n    confirmed : Boolean\nend\n\nclass Bill\nattributes\n    price : Real\n    billId : Integer\nend\n\nclass FreeRoomTypesDTO\nattributes\n    roomTypeDescription : String\n    numBeds : Integer\n    pricePerNight : Real\n    numFreeRooms : Integer\nend\n\nclass Customer\nattributes\n    firstName : String\n    lastName : String\nend\n\nclass RoomReservation\nattributes\n    checkInDate : String\n    checkOutDate : String\nend\n\nclass RoomExtra\nattributes\n    price : Real\n    description : String\nend\n\nclass Room\nattributes\n    numBeds : Integer\n    pricePerNight : Real\n    occupied : Boolean\n    blocked : Boolean\n    roomNumber : Integer\nend\n\nassociation BookingBill between\n    Booking [1]\n    Bill [0..*] role bills\nend\n\nassociation BookingCustomer between\n    Booking [1]\n    Customer [1] role customer\nend\n\nassociation BookingRoomReservation between\n    Booking [1]\n    RoomReservation [*] role roomReservations\nend\n\nassociation RoomReservationExtras between\n    RoomReservation [1]\n    RoomExtra [*] role roomExtras\nend\n\nassociation RoomReservationRoom between\n    RoomReservation [1]\n    Room [*] role room\nend\n\nassociation BillRoomReservation between\n    Bill [1]\n    RoomReservation [1] role roomReservation\nend\n\nconstraints\ncontext Bill inv positiveBill:\n    self.price >= 0\n\ncontext Bill inv uniqueBillId:\n    Bill.allInstances->isUnique(b | b.billId)\n\ncontext Room inv positiveNumBeds:\n    self.numBeds > 0\n</domain_model>\n"
}, {

So it can be used as follows:\n!store4.location := Location(14.0, 289.0)\n\n-- Enums usage:\n    -- enum Type { Clothes, Shoes }\n-- Can be used as follows:\n!store4.type := #Clothes\n</syntax_reference>\n"

"content" : "Given a conceptual model expressed in the UML-based Specification Environment (USE), your task is to generate valid and realistic instances that conform to the provided model.\n<requirements>\n- Instances must be syntactically correct according to the USE syntax_reference.\n- Avoid unnecessary comments and output the instance in plain text (i.e., not markdown).\n- Make sure instances fulfill all the model's constraints, and that multiplicities, relationships, and attributes are valid and realistic.\n- Provide multiple instances with diverse data values and structure.\n</requirements>\n\n<syntax_reference>\nHere there is a snippet showing how to create objects and set values in the specific .soil language required:\n-- This is a comment example\n\n-- Primitive data types:\n    -- Integer i.e. 1, 2, 3, etc.\n    -- Real i.e. 1.0, 21.89, 322.05556, etc.\n    -- Boolean i.e. true or false\n    -- String i.e. 'Hello World'\n\n-- You can create instances with the following syntax:\n!new <instance type>('<instance name>')\n-- Example:\n!new Client('client1')\n!new Store('store4')\n\n-- You can assign values to attributes for a created instance with the following syntax:\n!<instance name>.<attribute name> := <value>\n-- Example for different data types:\n!client1.clientId := 1 -- For Integer\n!client1.balance := 1123.45 -- For Real\n!client1.name := 'John' -- For Strings\n!store4.available := true -- For Boolean\n\n-- You can create associations between instances with the following syntax:\n!insert (<instance name1>, <instance name2>) into <association name>\n-- Example:\n!insert (client1, store4) into ClientStore\n\n-- Custom data types usage:\n    -- dataType Location\n    -- operations\n    --   Location(x : Real, y : Real)\n    --   some other operations\n    -- end\n-- You can create custom data types by calling the constructor directly; in this case, the constructor of Location requires two arguments: x and y of type Real. So it can be used as follows:\n!store4.location := Location(14.0, 289.0)\n\n-- Enums usage:\n    -- enum Type { Clothes, Shoes }\n-- Can be used as follows:\n!store4.type := #Clothes\n</syntax_reference>\n"

"content" : "Given a conceptual model expressed in the UML-based Specification Environment (USE), your task is to generate valid and realistic instances that conform to the provided model.\n<requirements>\n- Instances must be syntactically correct according to the USE syntax_reference.\n- Avoid unnecessary comments and output the instance in plain text (i.e., not markdown).\n- Make sure instances fulfill all the model's constraints, and that multiplicities, relationships, and attributes are valid and realistic.\n- Provide multiple instances with diverse data values and structure.\n</requirements>\n\n<syntax_reference>\nHere there is a snippet showing how to create objects and set values in the specific .soil language required:\n-- This is a comment example\n\n-- Primitive data types:\n    -- Integer i.e. 1, 2, 3, etc.\n    -- Real i.e. 1.0, 21.89, 322.05556, etc.\n    -- Boolean i.e. true or false\n    -- String i.e. 'Hello World'\n\n-- You can create instances with the following syntax:\n!new <instance type>('<instance name>')\n-- Example:\n!new Client('client1')\n!new Store('store4')\n\n-- You can assign values to attributes for a created instance with the following syntax:\n!<instance name>.<attribute name> := <value>\n-- Example for different data types:\n!client1.clientId := 1 -- For Integer\n!client1.balance := 1123.45 -- For Real\n!client1.name := 'John' -- For Strings\n!store4.available := true -- For Boolean\n\n-- You can create associations between instances with the following syntax:\n!insert (<instance name1>, <instance name2>) into <association name>\n-- Example:\n!insert (client1, store4) into ClientStore\n\n-- Custom data types usage:\n    -- dataType Location\n    -- operations\n    --   Location(x : Real, y : Real)\n    --   some other operations\n    -- end\n-- You can create custom data types by calling the constructor directly; in this case, the constructor of Location requires two arguments: x and y of type Real. So it can be used as follows:\n!store4.location := Location(14.0, 289.0)\n\n-- Enums usage:\n    -- enum Type { Clothes, Shoes }\n-- Can be used as follows:\n!store4.type := #Clothes\n</syntax_reference>\n"

"content" : "Create an instance for the following domain model:\n<domain_model>\nmodel HotelManagement\n\nclass Booking\nattributes\n    startDate : String\n    endDate : String\n    canceled : Boolean\n    bookingId : Integer\n    confirmed : Boolean\nend\n\nclass Bill\nattributes\n    price : Real\n    billId : Integer\nend\n\nclass FreeRoomTypesDTO\nattributes\n    roomTypeDescription : String\n    numBeds : Integer\n    pricePerNight : Real\n    numFreeRooms : Integer\nend\n\nclass Customer\nattributes\n    firstName : String\n    lastName : String\nend\n\nclass RoomReservation\nattributes\n    checkInDate : String\n    checkOutDate : String\nend\n\nclass RoomExtra\nattributes\n    price : Real\n    description : String\nend\n\nclass Room\nattributes\n    numBeds : Integer\n    pricePerNight : Real\n    occupied : Boolean\n    blocked : Boolean\n    roomNumber : Integer\nend\n\nassociation BookingBill between\n    Booking [1]\n    Bill [0..*] role bills\nend\n\nassociation BookingCustomer between\n    Booking [1]\n    Customer [1] role customer\nend\n\nassociation BookingRoomReservation between\n    Booking [1]\n    RoomReservation [*] role roomReservations\nend\n\nassociation RoomReservationExtras between\n    RoomReservation [1]\n    RoomExtra [*] role roomExtras\nend\n\nassociation RoomReservationRoom between\n    RoomReservation [1]\n    Room [*] role room\nend\n\nassociation BillRoomReservation between\n    Bill [1]\n    RoomReservation [1] role roomReservation\nend\n\nconstraints\ncontext Bill inv positiveBill:\n    self.price >= 0\n\ncontext Bill inv uniqueBillId:\n    Bill.allInstances->isUnique(b | b.billId)\n\ncontext Room inv positiveNumBeds:\n    self.numBeds > 0\n</domain_model>\n"

So it can be used as follows:\n!store4.location := Location(14.0, 289.0)\n\n-- Enums usage:\n    -- enum Type { Clothes, Shoes }\n-- Can be used as follows:\n!store4.type := #Clothes\n</syntax_reference>\n"

"content" : "Create an instance for the following domain model:\n<domain_model>\nmodel HotelManagement\n\nclass Booking\nattributes\n    startDate : String\n    endDate : String\n    canceled : Boolean\n    bookingId : Integer\n    confirmed : Boolean\nend\n\nclass Bill\nattributes\n    price : Real\n    billId : Integer\nend\n\nclass FreeRoomTypesDTO\nattributes\n    roomTypeDescription : String\n    numBeds : Integer\n    pricePerNight : Real\n    numFreeRooms : Integer\nend\n\nclass Customer\nattributes\n    firstName : String\n    lastName : String\nend\n\nclass RoomReservation\nattributes\n    checkInDate : String\n    checkOutDate : String\nend\n\nclass RoomExtra\nattributes\n    price : Real\n    description : String\nend\n\nclass Room\nattributes\n    numBeds : Integer\n    pricePerNight : Real\n    occupied : Boolean\n    blocked : Boolean\n    roomNumber : Integer\nend\n\nassociation BookingBill between\n    Booking [1]\n    Bill [0..*] role bills\nend\n\nassociation BookingCustomer between\n    Booking [1]\n    Customer [1] role customer\nend\n\nassociation BookingRoomReservation between\n    Booking [1]\n    RoomReservation [*] role roomReservations\nend\n\nassociation RoomReservationExtras between\n    RoomReservation [1]\n    RoomExtra [*] role roomExtras\nend\n\nassociation RoomReservationRoom between\n    RoomReservation [1]\n    Room [*] role room\nend\n\nassociation BillRoomReservation between\n    Bill [1]\n    RoomReservation [1] role roomReservation\nend\n\nconstraints\ncontext Bill inv positiveBill:\n    self.price >= 0\n\ncontext Bill inv uniqueBillId:\n    Bill.allInstances->isUnique(b | b.billId)\n\ncontext Room inv positiveNumBeds:\n    self.numBeds > 0\n</domain_model>\n"

"content" : "Given a conceptual model expressed in the UML-based Specification Environment (USE), your task is to generate valid and realistic instances that conform to the provided model.\n<requirements>\n- Instances must be syntactically correct according to the USE syntax_reference.\n- Avoid unnecessary comments and output the instance in plain text (i.e., not markdown).\n- Make sure instances fulfill all the model's constraints, and that multiplicities, relationships, and attributes are valid and realistic.\n- Provide multiple instances with diverse data values and structure.\n</requirements>\n\n<syntax_reference>\nHere there is a snippet showing how to create objects and set values in the specific .soil language required:\n-- This is a comment example\n\n-- Primitive data types:\n    -- Integer i.e. 1, 2, 3, etc.\n    -- Real i.e. 1.0, 21.89, 322.05556, etc.\n    -- Boolean i.e. true or false\n    -- String i.e. 'Hello World'\n\n-- You can create instances with the following syntax:\n!new <instance type>('<instance name>')\n-- Example:\n!new Client('client1')\n!new Store('store4')\n\n-- You can assign values to attributes for a created instance with the following syntax:\n!<instance name>.<attribute name> := <value>\n-- Example for different data types:\n!client1.clientId := 1 -- For Integer\n!client1.balance := 1123.45 -- For Real\n!client1.name := 'John' -- For Strings\n!store4.available := true -- For Boolean\n\n-- You can create associations between instances with the following syntax:\n!insert (<instance name1>, <instance name2>) into <association name>\n-- Example:\n!insert (client1, store4) into ClientStore\n\n-- Custom data types usage:\n    -- dataType Location\n    -- operations\n    --   Location(x : Real, y : Real)\n    --   some other operations\n    -- end\n-- You can create custom data types by calling the constructor directly; in this case, the constructor of Location requires two arguments: x and y of type Real. So it can be used as follows:\n!store4.location := Location(14.0, 289.0)\n\n-- Enums usage:\n    -- enum Type { Clothes, Shoes }\n-- Can be used as follows:\n!store4.type := #Clothes\n</syntax_reference>\n"
}, {

"content" : "Create an instance for the following domain model:\n<domain_model>\nmodel HotelManagement\n\nclass Booking\nattributes\n    startDate : String\n    endDate : String\n    canceled : Boolean\n    bookingId : Integer\n    confirmed : Boolean\nend\n\nclass Bill\nattributes\n    price : Real\n    billId : Integer\nend\n\nclass FreeRoomTypesDTO\nattributes\n    roomTypeDescription : String\n    numBeds : Integer\n    pricePerNight : Real\n    numFreeRooms : Integer\nend\n\nclass Customer\nattributes\n    firstName : String\n    lastName : String\nend\n\nclass RoomReservation\nattributes\n    checkInDate : String\n    checkOutDate : String\nend\n\nclass RoomExtra\nattributes\n    price : Real\n    description : String\nend\n\nclass Room\nattributes\n    numBeds : Integer\n    pricePerNight : Real\n    occupied : Boolean\n    blocked : Boolean\n    roomNumber : Integer\nend\n\nassociation BookingBill between\n    Booking [1]\n    Bill [0..*] role bills\nend\n\nassociation BookingCustomer between\n    Booking [1]\n    Customer [1] role customer\nend\n\nassociation BookingRoomReservation between\n    Booking [1]\n    RoomReservation [*] role roomReservations\nend\n\nassociation RoomReservationExtras between\n    RoomReservation [1]\n    RoomExtra [*] role roomExtras\nend\n\nassociation RoomReservationRoom between\n    RoomReservation [1]\n    Room [*] role room\nend\n\nassociation BillRoomReservation between\n    Bill [1]\n    RoomReservation [1] role roomReservation\nend\n\nconstraints\ncontext Bill inv positiveBill:\n    self.price >= 0\n\ncontext Bill inv uniqueBillId:\n    Bill.allInstances->isUnique(b | b.billId)\n\ncontext Room inv positiveNumBeds:\n    self.numBeds > 0\n</domain_model>\n"
}, {

"content" : "Given a conceptual model expressed in the UML-based Specification Environment (USE), your task is to generate valid and realistic instances that conform to the provided model.\n<requirements>\n- Instances must be syntactically correct according to the USE syntax_reference.\n- Avoid unnecessary comments and output the instance in plain text (i.e., not markdown).\n- Make sure instances fulfill all the model's constraints, and that multiplicities, relationships, and attributes are valid and realistic.\n- Provide multiple instances with diverse data values and structure.\n</requirements>\n\n<syntax_reference>\nHere there is a snippet showing how to create objects and set values in the specific .soil language required:\n-- This is a comment example\n\n-- Primitive data types:\n    -- Integer i.e. 1, 2, 3, etc.\n    -- Real i.e. 1.0, 21.89, 322.05556, etc.\n    -- Boolean i.e. true or false\n    -- String i.e. 'Hello World'\n\n-- You can create instances with the following syntax:\n!new <instance type>('<instance name>')\n-- Example:\n!new Client('client1')\n!new Store('store4')\n\n-- You can assign values to attributes for a created instance with the following syntax:\n!<instance name>.<attribute name> := <value>\n-- Example for different data types:\n!client1.clientId := 1 -- For Integer\n!client1.balance := 1123.45 -- For Real\n!client1.name := 'John' -- For Strings\n!store4.available := true -- For Boolean\n\n-- You can create associations between instances with the following syntax:\n!insert (<instance name1>, <instance name2>) into <association name>\n-- Example:\n!insert (client1, store4) into ClientStore\n\n-- Custom data types usage:\n    -- dataType Location\n    -- operations\n    --   Location(x : Real, y : Real)\n    --   some other operations\n    -- end\n-- You can create custom data types by calling the constructor directly; in this case, the constructor of Location requires two arguments: x and y of type Real. So it can be used as follows:\n!store4.location := Location(14.0, 289.0)\n\n-- Enums usage:\n    -- enum Type { Clothes, Shoes }\n-- Can be used as follows:\n!store4.type := #Clothes\n</syntax_reference>\n"

"content" : "Given a conceptual model expressed in the UML-based Specification Environment (USE), your task is to generate valid and realistic instances that conform to the provided model.\n<requirements>\n- Instances must be syntactically correct according to the USE syntax_reference.\n- Avoid unnecessary comments and output the instance in plain text (i.e., not markdown).\n- Make sure instances fulfill all the model's constraints, and that multiplicities, relationships, and attributes are valid and realistic.\n- Provide multiple instances with diverse data values and structure.\n</requirements>\n\n<syntax_reference>\nHere there is a snippet showing how to create objects and set values in the specific .soil language required:\n-- This is a comment example\n\n-- Primitive data types:\n    -- Integer i.e. 1, 2, 3, etc.\n    -- Real i.e. 1.0, 21.89, 322.05556, etc.\n    -- Boolean i.e. true or false\n    -- String i.e. 'Hello World'\n\n-- You can create instances with the following syntax:\n!new <instance type>('<instance name>')\n-- Example:\n!new Client('client1')\n!new Store('store4')\n\n-- You can assign values to attributes for a created instance with the following syntax:\n!<instance name>.<attribute name> := <value>\n-- Example for different data types:\n!client1.clientId := 1 -- For Integer\n!client1.balance := 1123.45 -- For Real\n!client1.name := 'John' -- For Strings\n!store4.available := true -- For Boolean\n\n-- You can create associations between instances with the following syntax:\n!insert (<instance name1>, <instance name2>) into <association name>\n-- Example:\n!insert (client1, store4) into ClientStore\n\n-- Custom data types usage:\n    -- dataType Location\n    -- operations\n    --   Location(x : Real, y : Real)\n    --   some other operations\n    -- end\n-- You can create custom data types by calling the constructor directly; in this case, the constructor of Location requires two arguments: x and y of type Real. So it can be used as follows:\n!store4.location := Location(14.0, 289.0)\n\n-- Enums usage:\n    -- enum Type { Clothes, Shoes }\n-- Can be used as follows:\n!store4.type := #Clothes\n</syntax_reference>\n"

So it can be used as follows:\n!store4.location := Location(14.0, 289.0)\n\n-- Enums usage:\n    -- enum Type { Clothes, Shoes }\n-- Can be used as follows:\n!store4.type := #Clothes\n</syntax_reference>\n"

"content" : "Given a conceptual model expressed in the UML-based Specification Environment (USE), your task is to generate valid and realistic instances that conform to the provided model.\n<requirements>\n- Instances must be syntactically correct according to the USE syntax_reference.\n- Avoid unnecessary comments and output the instance in plain text (i.e., not markdown).\n- Make sure instances fulfill all the model's constraints, and that multiplicities, relationships, and attributes are valid and realistic.\n- Provide multiple instances with diverse data values and structure.\n</requirements>\n\n<syntax_reference>\nHere there is a snippet showing how to create objects and set values in the specific .soil language required:\n-- This is a comment example\n\n-- Primitive data types:\n    -- Integer i.e. 1, 2, 3, etc.\n    -- Real i.e. 1.0, 21.89, 322.05556, etc.\n    -- Boolean i.e. true or false\n    -- String i.e. 'Hello World'\n\n-- You can create instances with the following syntax:\n!new <instance type>('<instance name>')\n-- Example:\n!new Client('client1')\n!new Store('store4')\n\n-- You can assign values to attributes for a created instance with the following syntax:\n!<instance name>.<attribute name> := <value>\n-- Example for different data types:\n!client1.clientId := 1 -- For Integer\n!client1.balance := 1123.45 -- For Real\n!client1.name := 'John' -- For Strings\n!store4.available := true -- For Boolean\n\n-- You can create associations between instances with the following syntax:\n!insert (<instance name1>, <instance name2>) into <association name>\n-- Example:\n!insert (client1, store4) into ClientStore\n\n-- Custom data types usage:\n    -- dataType Location\n    -- operations\n    --   Location(x : Real, y : Real)\n    --   some other operations\n    -- end\n-- You can create custom data types by calling the constructor directly; in this case, the constructor of Location requires two arguments: x and y of type Real. So it can be used as follows:\n!store4.location := Location(14.0, 289.0)\n\n-- Enums usage:\n    -- enum Type { Clothes, Shoes }\n-- Can be used as follows:\n!store4.type := #Clothes\n</syntax_reference>\n"

"content" : "Given a conceptual model expressed in the UML-based Specification Environment (USE), your task is to generate valid and realistic instances that conform to the provided model.\n<requirements>\n- Instances must be syntactically correct according to the USE syntax_reference.\n- Avoid unnecessary comments and output the instance in plain text (i.e., not markdown).\n- Make sure instances fulfill all the model's constraints, and that multiplicities, relationships, and attributes are valid and realistic.\n- Provide multiple instances with diverse data values and structure.\n</requirements>\n\n<syntax_reference>\nHere there is a snippet showing how to create objects and set values in the specific .soil language required:\n-- This is a comment example\n\n-- Primitive data types:\n    -- Integer i.e. 1, 2, 3, etc.\n    -- Real i.e. 1.0, 21.89, 322.05556, etc.\n    -- Boolean i.e. true or false\n    -- String i.e. 'Hello World'\n\n-- You can create instances with the following syntax:\n!new <instance type>('<instance name>')\n-- Example:\n!new Client('client1')\n!new Store('store4')\n\n-- You can assign values to attributes for a created instance with the following syntax:\n!<instance name>.<attribute name> := <value>\n-- Example for different data types:\n!client1.clientId := 1 -- For Integer\n!client1.balance := 1123.45 -- For Real\n!client1.name := 'John' -- For Strings\n!store4.available := true -- For Boolean\n\n-- You can create associations between instances with the following syntax:\n!insert (<instance name1>, <instance name2>) into <association name>\n-- Example:\n!insert (client1, store4) into ClientStore\n\n-- Custom data types usage:\n    -- dataType Location\n    -- operations\n    --   Location(x : Real, y : Real)\n    --   some other operations\n    -- end\n-- You can create custom data types by calling the constructor directly; in this case, the constructor of Location requires two arguments: x and y of type Real. So it can be used as follows:\n!store4.location := Location(14.0, 289.0)\n\n-- Enums usage:\n    -- enum Type { Clothes, Shoes }\n-- Can be used as follows:\n!store4.type := #Clothes\n</syntax_reference>\n"

"content" : "Create an instance for the following domain model:\n<domain_model>\nmodel HotelManagement\n\nclass Booking\nattributes\n    startDate : String\n    endDate : String\n    canceled : Boolean\n    bookingId : Integer\n    confirmed : Boolean\nend\n\nclass Bill\nattributes\n    price : Real\n    billId : Integer\nend\n\nclass FreeRoomTypesDTO\nattributes\n    roomTypeDescription : String\n    numBeds : Integer\n    pricePerNight : Real\n    numFreeRooms : Integer\nend\n\nclass Customer\nattributes\n    firstName : String\n    lastName : String\nend\n\nclass RoomReservation\nattributes\n    checkInDate : String\n    checkOutDate : String\nend\n\nclass RoomExtra\nattributes\n    price : Real\n    description : String\nend\n\nclass Room\nattributes\n    numBeds : Integer\n    pricePerNight : Real\n    occupied : Boolean\n    blocked : Boolean\n    roomNumber : Integer\nend\n\nassociation BookingBill between\n    Booking [1]\n    Bill [0..*] role bills\nend\n\nassociation BookingCustomer between\n    Booking [1]\n    Customer [1] role customer\nend\n\nassociation BookingRoomReservation between\n    Booking [1]\n    RoomReservation [*] role roomReservations\nend\n\nassociation RoomReservationExtras between\n    RoomReservation [1]\n    RoomExtra [*] role roomExtras\nend\n\nassociation RoomReservationRoom between\n    RoomReservation [1]\n    Room [*] role room\nend\n\nassociation BillRoomReservation between\n    Bill [1]\n    RoomReservation [1] role roomReservation\nend\n\nconstraints\ncontext Bill inv positiveBill:\n    self.price >= 0\n\ncontext Bill inv uniqueBillId:\n    Bill.allInstances->isUnique(b | b.billId)\n\ncontext Room inv positiveNumBeds:\n    self.numBeds > 0\n</domain_model>\n"

23:54:18.982 [main] INFO  dev.langchain4j.http.client.log.LoggingHttpClient - HTTP request:
- method: POST

- headers: [Authorization: Beare...3d], [User-Agent: langchain4j-openai], [Content-Type: application/json]
- body: {
"model" : "openai/gpt-4o",
"messages" : [ {
"role" : "system",
"content" : "Given a conceptual model expressed in the UML-based Specification Environment (USE), your task is to generate valid and realistic instances that conform to the provided model.\n<requirements>\n- Instances must be syntactically correct according to the USE syntax_reference.\n- Avoid unnecessary comments and output the instance in plain text (i.e., not markdown).\n- Make sure instances fulfill all the model's constraints, and that multiplicities, relationships, and attributes are valid and realistic.\n- Provide multiple instances with diverse data values and structure.\n</requirements>\n\n<syntax_reference>\nHere there is a snippet showing how to create objects and set values in the specific .soil language required:\n-- This is a comment example\n\n-- Primitive data types:\n    -- Integer i.e. 1, 2, 3, etc.\n    -- Real i.e. 1.0, 21.89, 322.05556, etc.\n    -- Boolean i.e. true or false\n    -- String i.e. 'Hello World'\n\n-- You can create instances with the following syntax:\n!new <instance type>('<instance name>')\n-- Example:\n!new Client('client1')\n!new Store('store4')\n\n-- You can assign values to attributes for a created instance with the following syntax:\n!<instance name>.<attribute name> := <value>\n-- Example for different data types:\n!client1.clientId := 1 -- For Integer\n!client1.balance := 1123.45 -- For Real\n!client1.name := 'John' -- For Strings\n!store4.available := true -- For Boolean\n\n-- You can create associations between instances with the following syntax:\n!insert (<instance name1>, <instance name2>) into <association name>\n-- Example:\n!insert (client1, store4) into ClientStore\n\n-- Custom data types usage:\n    -- dataType Location\n    -- operations\n    --   Location(x : Real, y : Real)\n    --   some other operations\n    -- end\n-- You can create custom data types by calling the constructor directly; in this case, the constructor of Location requires two arguments: x and y of type Real. So it can be used as follows:\n!store4.location := Location(14.0, 289.0)\n\n-- Enums usage:\n    -- enum Type { Clothes, Shoes }\n-- Can be used as follows:\n!store4.type := #Clothes\n</syntax_reference>\n"
}, {
"role" : "user",
"content" : "Create an instance for the following domain model:\n<domain_model>\nmodel HotelManagement\n\nclass Booking\nattributes\n    startDate : String\n    endDate : String\n    canceled : Boolean\n    bookingId : Integer\n    confirmed : Boolean\nend\n\nclass Bill\nattributes\n    price : Real\n    billId : Integer\nend\n\nclass FreeRoomTypesDTO\nattributes\n    roomTypeDescription : String\n    numBeds : Integer\n    pricePerNight : Real\n    numFreeRooms : Integer\nend\n\nclass Customer\nattributes\n    firstName : String\n    lastName : String\nend\n\nclass RoomReservation\nattributes\n    checkInDate : String\n    checkOutDate : String\nend\n\nclass RoomExtra\nattributes\n    price : Real\n    description : String\nend\n\nclass Room\nattributes\n    numBeds : Integer\n    pricePerNight : Real\n    occupied : Boolean\n    blocked : Boolean\n    roomNumber : Integer\nend\n\nassociation BookingBill between\n    Booking [1]\n    Bill [0..*] role bills\nend\n\nassociation BookingCustomer between\n    Booking [1]\n    Customer [1] role customer\nend\n\nassociation BookingRoomReservation between\n    Booking [1]\n    RoomReservation [*] role roomReservations\nend\n\nassociation RoomReservationExtras between\n    RoomReservation [1]\n    RoomExtra [*] role roomExtras\nend\n\nassociation RoomReservationRoom between\n    RoomReservation [1]\n    Room [*] role room\nend\n\nassociation BillRoomReservation between\n    Bill [1]\n    RoomReservation [1] role roomReservation\nend\n\nconstraints\ncontext Bill inv positiveBill:\n    self.price >= 0\n\ncontext Bill inv uniqueBillId:\n    Bill.allInstances->isUnique(b | b.billId)\n\ncontext Room inv positiveNumBeds:\n    self.numBeds > 0\n</domain_model>\n"
}, {
